0How to score 180: the game plan

This book is ordered by marks. Read a chapter, do its self-test without looking, then drill the same topic in the app (Study → filter by section). Every chapter follows the same pattern: RULE (what to know), EXAMPLE, TRAP (how KEA tricks you) and MEMORY HOOK. Green boxes marked ASKED BY KEA are facts that appeared in KEA papers from 2024–26.

The exam in one table

PaperTimeQuestionsWhat is inside
Paper 1 · General Knowledge10:30–12:30100 × 1 markCurrent events, general awareness, Constitution, Indian & Karnataka history, geography, state administration, Karnataka economy (rural development, Panchayat Raj, co-operatives), environment. KEA also puts about 11 aptitude/reasoning questions here.
Paper 2 · Kannada, English, Computer14:30–16:30100 × 1 markQ1–35 Kannada · Q36–70 English · Q71–100 Computer (this split held in every KEA paper of this type from 2024 to 2026)
Your mark on a questionEffect
Correct+1
Wrong−0.25
5th circle shaded ("not answering")0
Row left blank (no circle)−0.25 — same as wrong

Target: 180 = 88 + 92

SectionQuestionsTargetHow
GK (Paper 1)10088Chapters 1–10. Aptitude block = 11 near-certain marks; Panchayat Raj/revenue/Constitution = 15–20 marks of pure memory.
Kannada3531Grammar is formula-like (Chapter 11). Literature is a list to memorise (Chapter 12).
English3534Your strongest area. Chapter 13–14 only fixes the traps.
Computer3027Chapter 15: shortcuts, Excel, networking ports, IT Act, DBMS.
Reality checkIn 2024 the best score in Bengaluru Urban was 145/200 (KEA's official score list). Aim for 180 so that a bad day still lands you above 145.

Negative marking: the maths that decides your rank

RuleA guess pays off whenever your chance of being right is above 20%. A blind guess among 4 options = +0.06 on average; rule out one option = +0.17; rule out two = +0.38. A blank row = −0.25, always.
  • Never leave a row blank. If you truly have no idea, shade the 5th circle (0) — or better, eliminate one option and guess.
  • Use the 5th circle only when an option looks like a deliberate trap pulling you in.
  • Multi-statement questions: decide the statement you are surest of first, then delete every option that contradicts it.
  • "How many of the above are correct?" questions cannot be eliminated — you must know each statement.
  • Words like only, always, never, all make a statement more likely false.
  • KEA sometimes prints options out of order (e.g. (1) (3) (2) (4) in two columns). Shade by the number, never by position.

Time plan inside each 120 minutes

MinutesPaper 1Paper 2
0–60Pass 1: answer every question you are sure of (about 40 s each); circle the doubtful ones on the question bookletEnglish Q36–70 first (25 min), then Computer Q71–100 (25 min)
60–100Pass 2: doubtful questions with elimination; do the aptitude block (Q51–61 region) carefullyKannada Q1–35 (40 min): grammar first, literature last
100–115Pass 3: check OMR bubbles against the booklet; version code and register numberSame
115–120 (+5)Fill every remaining row — guess after elimination or shade circle 5Same

1Indian Constitution and Polity

KEA asks 6–10 polity questions per GK paper: articles, amendments, bodies and the Panchayat articles (Chapter 2).

1.1 Making of the Constitution

ItemFact
Constituent Assembly set up underCabinet Mission Plan, 1946
First meeting9 December 1946 · temporary chairman Dr Sachchidananda Sinha
Permanent President of the AssemblyDr Rajendra Prasad (11 Dec 1946)
Objectives ResolutionJawaharlal Nehru, 13 Dec 1946 — became the Preamble
Drafting Committee chairmanDr B.R. Ambedkar (committee formed 29 Aug 1947)
Constitutional adviserSir B.N. Rau
Adopted26 November 1949 (Constitution Day / Samvidhan Divas)
Came into force26 January 1950 (chosen to honour Purna Swaraj Day, 26 Jan 1930)
Time taken2 years, 11 months, 18 days
Original size395 articles, 22 parts, 8 schedules (today 12 schedules)

1.2 Preamble

Rule"We, the people of India" — sovereignty lies with the people. India is a Sovereign, Socialist, Secular, Democratic Republic. Objectives: Justice (social, economic, political), Liberty (thought, expression, belief, faith, worship), Equality (status, opportunity), Fraternity (dignity of the individual, unity and integrity of the nation).
  • Amended only once: 42nd Amendment, 1976 added socialist, secular and integrity.
  • Kesavananda Bharati case (1973): the Preamble is part of the Constitution (Berubari, 1960, had said it was not).
  • Liberty, equality, fraternity: from the French Revolution. Justice (social, economic, political): from the Russian Revolution.

1.3 Parts you must know

PartSubjectArticles
IUnion and its territory1–4
IICitizenship5–11
IIIFundamental Rights12–35
IVDirective Principles of State Policy36–51
IV-AFundamental Duties51A
VThe Union (President, Parliament, Supreme Court, CAG)52–151
VIThe States (Governor, legislature, High Courts)152–237
VIIIUnion Territories239–242
IXPanchayats243–243O
IX-AMunicipalities243P–243ZG
IX-BCo-operative societies (97th Amendment, 2011)243ZH–243ZT
XICentre–State relations245–263
XIIFinance, property, contracts (Finance Commission Art 280; right to property Art 300A)264–300A
XIVServices; Public Service Commissions308–323
XVElections (Election Commission Art 324)324–329A
XVIIOfficial language (Hindi in Devanagari, Art 343)343–351
XVIIIEmergency provisions352–360
XXAmendment of the Constitution368

1.4 The 12 Schedules

ScheduleContents
1States and Union Territories
2Salaries/emoluments: President, Governors, Speakers, judges, CAG
3Forms of oaths and affirmations
4Allocation of Rajya Sabha seats to states/UTs
5Administration of Scheduled Areas and Scheduled Tribes
6Tribal areas of Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram
7Union List, State List, Concurrent List (originally 97, 66 and 47 subjects)
822 languages (Kannada is one)
9Laws protected from judicial review (added by the 1st Amendment, 1951 — land reform laws)
10Anti-defection (52nd Amendment, 1985)
11Panchayats: 29 subjects (73rd Amendment)
12Municipalities: 18 subjects (74th Amendment)
Memory hook11 ↔ villages (eleven players in a village cricket team, 29 subjects); 12 ↔ cities (18 subjects).

1.5 Borrowed features

SourceBorrowed
Government of India Act 1935Federal scheme, office of Governor, emergency provisions, public service commissions — the largest single source
BritainParliamentary government, rule of law, single citizenship, cabinet system, writs, bicameralism
USAFundamental Rights, judicial review, independent judiciary, impeachment of President, Vice-President
IrelandDirective Principles, nomination of Rajya Sabha members, method of electing the President
CanadaFederation with a strong Centre, residuary powers with the Centre, Governor appointed by the Centre
AustraliaConcurrent List, joint sitting of Parliament, freedom of trade
Former USSRFundamental Duties; ideal of justice (social, economic, political)
Germany (Weimar)Suspension of Fundamental Rights during emergency
FranceRepublic; liberty, equality, fraternity
South AfricaProcedure for amendment; election of Rajya Sabha members
Japan"Procedure established by law"

1.6 Fundamental Rights (Part III)

RightArticlesKey points
Equality14–1814 equality before law; 15 no discrimination; 16 equal opportunity in public jobs; 17 untouchability abolished; 18 titles abolished
Freedom19–2219: six freedoms — speech, assembly, association (incl. co-operatives), movement, residence, profession; 20 protection in conviction; 21 life and personal liberty; 21A free education 6–14 years (86th Amendment, 2002); 22 arrest and detention
Against exploitation23–2423 no human trafficking/forced labour; 24 no child below 14 in factories, mines, hazardous work
Religion25–28Freedom of conscience; manage religious affairs; no tax for a religion; no religious instruction in state schools
Cultural and educational29–30Protect language/script/culture; minorities can run educational institutions
Constitutional remedies32Go directly to the Supreme Court — Ambedkar called it "the heart and soul of the Constitution"
The five writsHabeas corpus "produce the body" (illegal detention) · Mandamus "we command" (a public official must do a duty) · Prohibition stops a lower court acting beyond its jurisdiction (before the order) · Certiorari quashes an order already passed by a lower court · Quo warranto "by what authority" (someone holding a public office illegally). Supreme Court issues them under Art 32, High Courts under Art 226 (wider: also for legal rights).
TrapThe right to property is not a Fundamental Right. The 44th Amendment (1978) removed it; it is now a legal right under Art 300A. During a national emergency, Articles 20 and 21 can never be suspended (44th Amendment).

1.7 Directive Principles (Part IV, Art 36–51)

  • Not enforceable in court (Art 37) but "fundamental in the governance of the country". Borrowed from Ireland.
  • Gandhian: Art 40 organise village panchayats · Art 43 cottage industries · Art 43B co-operative societies (97th) · Art 46 weaker sections · Art 47 prohibition of intoxicating drinks and improving nutrition · Art 48 ban cow slaughter, improve breeds.
  • Socialist: Art 38 social order · 39 equal pay, no concentration of wealth · 39A free legal aid (42nd) · 41 right to work, education, public assistance · 42 maternity relief · 43 living wage · 43A workers in management (42nd).
  • Liberal: Art 44 Uniform Civil Code · 45 early childhood care up to age 6 (after 86th) · 48A environment, forests, wildlife (42nd) · 49 monuments · 50 separate judiciary from executive · 51 international peace.

1.8 Fundamental Duties (Art 51A)

  • Added by the 42nd Amendment (1976) on the Swaran Singh Committee's recommendation; idea from the USSR.
  • Originally 10; the 86th Amendment (2002) added the 11th: parents/guardians must provide education to children aged 6–14.
  • Include: respect the Constitution, flag and anthem; uphold sovereignty, unity and integrity; defend the country; promote harmony and renounce practices derogatory to women; preserve composite culture; protect the environment; develop scientific temper; safeguard public property; strive for excellence.

1.9 Union executive

OfficeKey facts
President (Art 52)Elected by an electoral college (Art 54): elected MPs of both Houses + elected MLAs of states, Delhi and Puducherry. Term 5 years; minimum age 35. Removed by impeachment (Art 61) for violating the Constitution. Pardoning power Art 72; ordinances Art 123. First: Dr Rajendra Prasad; first woman: Pratibha Patil; current: Droupadi Murmu (15th, since July 2022).
Vice-President (Art 63)Ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha (Art 64); elected by members of both Houses (Art 66); age 35; 5 years.
Prime Minister and Council of Ministers (Art 74–75)Aid and advise the President; collectively responsible to the Lok Sabha. Size capped at 15% of the Lok Sabha (91st Amendment, 2003).
Attorney General (Art 76)Highest law officer; can speak in Parliament but cannot vote.
CAG (Art 148)"Guardian of the public purse"; removed like a Supreme Court judge; reports go to the President, who lays them before Parliament.

1.10 Parliament (Art 79: President + Lok Sabha + Rajya Sabha)

Lok SabhaRajya Sabha
Maximum strength550 (530 states + 20 UTs) since the 104th Amendment ended Anglo-Indian nomination; 543 elected seats today250 (238 elected + 12 nominated for literature, science, art, social service)
Term5 years (can be dissolved)Permanent; one-third retire every 2 years; member's term 6 years
Minimum age2530
Presiding officerSpeaker (Art 93) — decides whether a bill is a Money BillVice-President as Chairman
Karnataka's seats2812
  • Money Bill (Art 110): only in the Lok Sabha; Rajya Sabha can only delay it 14 days.
  • Joint sitting (Art 108): presided by the Speaker; not for Money Bills or constitutional amendments. Held three times: 1961 (Dowry Prohibition), 1978 (Banking Service Commission repeal), 2002 (POTA).
  • Budget = "Annual Financial Statement" (Art 112). Zero Hour is an Indian innovation (1962). No-confidence motions only in the Lok Sabha.

1.11 Judiciary

  • Supreme Court (Art 124): CJI + up to 33 judges (34 total). Retire at 65. Removed by Parliament (special majority) for proved misbehaviour or incapacity.
  • Jurisdiction: original (Art 131, Centre–state disputes), writs (Art 32), appeals, advisory (Art 143, President's reference).
  • High Courts (Art 214): judges retire at 62; writs under Art 226. Karnataka High Court: Bengaluru, with benches at Dharwad and Kalaburagi.
  • Public Interest Litigation was pioneered by Justices P.N. Bhagwati and V.R. Krishna Iyer. Lok Adalats work under the Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987.

1.12 State government and Karnataka

  • Governor (Art 153): appointed by the President (Art 155), holds office at the President's pleasure (5-year term), age 35; ordinances Art 213. Karnataka's Governor: Thaawarchand Gehlot (since 2021).
  • Chief Minister and ministers: Art 164. Advocate General: Art 165.
  • Karnataka is bicameral: Legislative Assembly (Vidhana Sabha) 224 seats; Legislative Council (Vidhana Parishat) 75. Six states have councils: Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Maharashtra, Bihar, Uttar Pradesh. Art 169: Parliament creates/abolishes a council on the assembly's resolution.
  • Vidhana Soudha, Bengaluru (completed 1956, CM Kengal Hanumanthaiah). Suvarna Vidhana Soudha, Belagavi hosts the winter session.

1.13 Emergency provisions

ArticleTypeFacts
352National emergencyWar, external aggression or armed rebellion (replaced "internal disturbance" by the 44th Amendment). Declared 1962, 1971, 1975–77.
356President's RuleFailure of constitutional machinery in a state. S.R. Bommai case (1994), which came from Karnataka, limited its misuse.
360Financial emergencyNever declared.

1.14 Constitutional and statutory bodies

BodyBasisRemember
Election CommissionArt 324Founded 25 Jan 1950 (National Voters' Day). Multi-member body. CEC removed like an SC judge.
State Election CommissionArt 243K (panchayats), 243ZA (municipalities)Conducts local body elections
Finance CommissionArt 280Every 5 years; shares central taxes with states. 16th Finance Commission: chairman Arvind Panagariya
State Finance CommissionArt 243IEvery 5 years; funds for panchayats
UPSC / State PSCs (KPSC)Art 315Recruitment for civil services
National Commission for SCs / STs / Backward ClassesArt 338 / 338A (89th, 2003) / 338B (102nd, 2018)Protect the rights of these groups
GST CouncilArt 279AChaired by the Union Finance Minister
NITI AayogExecutive resolution, 1 Jan 2015Replaced the Planning Commission (1950); PM is chairperson
NHRCProtection of Human Rights Act 1993Statutory
LokpalLokpal and Lokayuktas Act 2013First Lokpal: Justice P.C. Ghose (2019)
Karnataka LokayuktaKarnataka Lokayukta Act 1984State anti-corruption ombudsman
Right to InformationRTI Act 2005Reply within 30 days (48 hours if life/liberty is involved)

1.15 Amendments KEA likes

AmendmentYearWhat it did
1st1951Added the 9th Schedule (protects land reform laws)
7th1956Reorganised states on linguistic lines
42nd ("mini-constitution")1976Socialist, secular, integrity; Fundamental Duties; Art 39A, 43A, 48A
44th1978Removed right to property from FRs (Art 300A); "armed rebellion" for emergency
52nd1985Anti-defection law, 10th Schedule
61st1989Voting age lowered from 21 to 18
73rd / 74th1992 (in force 1993)Panchayats / municipalities
86th2002Art 21A education 6–14; 11th Fundamental Duty
91st2003Council of Ministers ≤ 15% of the House; tighter anti-defection
97th2011Co-operative societies: Art 19(1)(c), Art 43B, Part IX-B
101st2016GST (in force 1 July 2017)
102nd2018Constitutional status for the National Commission for Backward Classes
103rd201910% reservation for economically weaker sections (EWS)
104th2019/2020Ended Anglo-Indian nomination; extended SC/ST seat reservation
106th2023One-third of seats for women in Lok Sabha and state assemblies (Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam)

1.16 Citizenship and languages

  • Art 5–11; Parliament regulates citizenship (Art 11). Citizenship Act 1955: acquired by birth, descent, registration, naturalisation, incorporation of territory.
  • Official language: Hindi in Devanagari, international form of numerals (Art 343).
  • 8th Schedule: 22 languages (originally 14). Added: Sindhi (1967), Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali (1992), Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santali (2003).
  • Classical languages: Tamil (2004), Sanskrit (2005), Telugu and Kannada (2008), Malayalam (2013), Odia (2014); Marathi, Pali, Prakrit, Assamese and Bengali (2024).
Self-test
  1. Which Article deals with the Uniform Civil Code? (1) 40 (2) 44 (3) 48A (4) 51A
  2. Fundamental Duties were added on the recommendation of: (1) Sarkaria Commission (2) Swaran Singh Committee (3) Balwantrai Mehta (4) Kothari Commission
  3. Which writ is issued to release a person from illegal detention? (1) Mandamus (2) Certiorari (3) Habeas corpus (4) Quo warranto
  4. Karnataka Legislative Council has how many members? (1) 224 (2) 75 (3) 28 (4) 12
  5. The 61st Amendment: (1) GST (2) voting age 18 (3) anti-defection (4) EWS quota
  6. Right to property today is: (1) a Fundamental Right (2) a legal right under Art 300A (3) a Directive Principle (4) abolished completely
  7. Who decides whether a bill is a Money Bill? (1) President (2) Finance Minister (3) Speaker of the Lok Sabha (4) Chairman of the Rajya Sabha
  8. Which Article cannot be suspended during a national emergency? (1) 19 (2) 21 (3) 14 (4) 32
  9. Part IX-B of the Constitution deals with: (1) Municipalities (2) Co-operative societies (3) Tribunals (4) Panchayats
  10. Free legal aid is a Directive Principle under: (1) Art 39A (2) Art 41 (3) Art 45 (4) Art 50

Answers: 1-2, 2-2, 3-3, 4-2, 5-2, 6-2, 7-3, 8-2, 9-2, 10-1

2Panchayat Raj, Rural Development and Co-operatives

This is the VAO's own world. The notification lists "rural development, Panchayat Raj and rural co-operatives" by name, and every KEA paper of 2025–26 asked several questions here.

2.1 Constitution, Part IX (73rd Amendment, 1992)

RulePassed 1992, in force 24 April 1993 (National Panchayati Raj Day). Articles 243 to 243-O. 11th Schedule = 29 subjects. Root: Article 40 (Directive Principle: organise village panchayats).
ArticleProvision
243Definitions (Gram Sabha, panchayat area, village)
243AGram Sabha — all registered voters of the village
243BThree tiers: village, intermediate, district. The intermediate tier is optional if the state's population is 20 lakh or less
243CComposition; members elected directly
243DReservation: SC/ST in proportion to population; women at least one-third (including chairpersons)
243ETerm 5 years; election before expiry; if dissolved, election within 6 months
243FDisqualifications; minimum age 21
243GPowers and responsibilities (11th Schedule)
243HPower to levy taxes, funds
243IState Finance Commission every 5 years (set up by the Governor)
243JAudit of accounts
243KState Election Commission (appointed by the Governor; removed only like a High Court judge)
243MNot applicable to Scheduled Areas and some tribal states (extended to Scheduled Areas by PESA Act 1996)
243OCourts cannot interfere in panchayat electoral matters

74th Amendment: municipalities, Part IX-A, 243P–243ZG, 12th Schedule = 18 subjects, in force 1 June 1993.

2.2 Committees, in order

CommitteeYearRecommended
Balwantrai Mehta1957Three-tier "democratic decentralisation" (to review the Community Development Programme 1952 and National Extension Service 1953). First Panchayat Raj: Nagaur, Rajasthan, 2 Oct 1959 (inaugurated by Nehru); Andhra Pradesh followed
Ashok Mehta1977Two-tier system (Zilla Parishad + Mandal Panchayat)
G.V.K. Rao1985District as the basic unit of planning
L.M. Singhvi1986Constitutional status for panchayats
Memory hookB-A-G-L in time order: Balwantrai 57 → Ashok 77 → GVK 85 → L.M. Singhvi 86.

2.3 Panchayat Raj in Karnataka

  • 1983 Act created Zilla Parishads and Mandal Panchayats; elections held in 1987. CM Ramakrishna Hegde, RDPR minister Abdul Nazir Sab ("Neersab", known for rural drinking water).
  • Karnataka Panchayat Raj Act, 1993 — renamed the Karnataka Gram Swaraj and Panchayat Raj Act, 1993 by the 2015 amendment.
  • Three tiers: Gram Panchayat (executive officer: Panchayat Development Officer, PDO) → Taluk Panchayat (Executive Officer) → Zilla Panchayat (Chief Executive Officer, an IAS officer). Adhyaksha and Upadhyaksha are elected by the members.
  • Karnataka reserves 50% of panchayat seats for women (the Constitution only requires one-third).
  • Training: Abdul Nazir Sab State Institute of Rural Development and Panchayat Raj (ANSSIRD&PR), Mysuru. National: NIRDPR, Hyderabad.
  • Elections: Karnataka State Election Commission (Art 243K). Funds: Karnataka State Finance Commission (Art 243I).
Trap"Women get at least half the seats under the Constitution" — false (one-third). Gram Sabha is 243A (not 243B/243G). Nagaur is in Rajasthan.

ProgrammeYearKey fact

Asked by KEA (22 Feb 2026)VB-G RAM G = Viksit Bharat — Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Gramin), the new rural employment guarantee framework.

2.5 Co-operatives

FactDetail
India's first co-operative lawCo-operative Credit Societies Act, 1904; broader Act in 1912

Karnataka lawKarnataka Co-operative Societies Act, 1959; Karnataka Souharda Sahakari Act, 1997 (self-reliant co-operatives, minimal state control)
Constitution97th Amendment (2011): forming co-operatives became a Fundamental Right under Art 19(1)(c); Art 43B (DPSP); Part IX-B
Union Ministry of Co-operationCreated July 2021
Short-term credit structurePACS (village) → District Central Co-operative Bank (DCCB) → State Co-operative (Apex) Bank

DairyAmul (Anand, Gujarat, 1946); Verghese Kurien — "father of the White Revolution" (Operation Flood, 1970). Karnataka Milk Federation (KMF) — brand Nandini

InternationalThe UN declared 2025 the International Year of Co-operatives; International Day of Co-operatives: first Saturday of July

3Revenue Administration and Land Records

This is the job you are applying for. Recent KEA papers asked about land revenue systems, land reforms, RTC and revenue terms — easy marks if you know the vocabulary.

3.1 The revenue chain in Karnataka

LevelOfficerNote
StateRevenue Minister · Revenue Department (Principal Secretary)Also Survey, Settlement and Land Records (SSLR) department
Division (4)Regional CommissionerBengaluru, Mysuru, Belagavi, Kalaburagi
DistrictDeputy Commissioner (District Collector and District Magistrate)Appointing authority for VAOs
Sub-divisionAssistant CommissionerSub-Divisional Magistrate
TalukTahsildarTaluk Executive Magistrate; heads the taluk office
Hobli (group of villages)Revenue Inspector (RI)Supervises VAOs; Nadakacheri at hobli level
Village / circleVillage Administrative Officer (formerly Village Accountant)Keeps village land and revenue records
VillageGrama Sahayaka (village assistant)Helps the VAO

History: the Karnataka Village Offices Abolition Act, 1961 ended hereditary village offices (Patel, Shanbhog); salaried village accountants replaced the Shanbhog.

3.2 What a VAO does

  • Maintains village records: RTC (Pahani), mutation register, land revenue demand and collection, village maps.
  • Crop survey and crop loss assessment after floods/drought; reports disasters.
  • Field verification for land grants, encroachments, mutation, caste/income/residence certificates.
  • Helps with elections, census and government schemes in the village.

3.3 Land record vocabulary (learn every line)

TermMeaning
RTC / PahaniRecord of Rights, Tenancy and Crops — owner, tenant, area, crops and liabilities of a survey number
Mutation (MR)Updating ownership in the records after sale, inheritance, partition or gift
KhataThe property/revenue account in an owner's name
Pouthi khateTransferring the khata to the legal heirs after the owner's death
Survey number · Hissa numberNumber of a land parcel · a sub-division of a survey number
Podi (Phodi)Sub-dividing a survey number into hissas
AkarbandRegister of area and assessment (revenue) for each survey number
TippanField measurement sketch prepared during survey
11E sketchSurvey sketch required for sub-division/transfer of part of a survey number
Kandaya (ಕಂದಾಯ)Land revenue
JamabandiAnnual audit/settlement of village accounts and land revenue
GomalaLand reserved for grazing village cattle
Kharab landLand not assessed to revenue: "A" kharab = unfit for cultivation within a holding; "B" kharab = reserved for public use (roads, tanks, burial grounds)
DarkhastApplication for grant of government land
Bagair hukumUnauthorised cultivation of government land (can be regularised under the rules)
Hakku patraTitle deed issued for a land/site grant
InamLand granted free of revenue by former rulers (abolished by Inam abolition laws)
Mahazar · PanchanamaSpot-inspection record signed by witnesses
Encumbrance Certificate (EC)From the Sub-Registrar: lists registered transactions/charges on a property
Land conversionPermission to use agricultural land for non-agricultural purposes (Deputy Commissioner)

3.4 Land units (Karnataka)

UnitEquals
1 acre40 guntas = 43,560 sq ft ≈ 4,047 m²
1 gunta1,089 sq ft (33 ft × 33 ft)
1 hectare10,000 m² ≈ 2.47 acres
1 km²100 hectares
ExampleA 2 acre 20 gunta plot = 2.5 acres ≈ 1.01 hectare.

3.5 Land laws and digital systems

Law / systemRemember
Karnataka Land Revenue Act, 1964Basic law for land revenue, records, mutation, grants, conversion
Karnataka Land Reforms Act, 1961 (in force 1965)1974 amendment under Devaraj Urs: "land to the tiller", tenancy abolished, land tribunals. 2020 amendment removed the ban on non-farmers buying farmland (Sections 79A, 79B)
Karnataka SC/ST (PTCL) Act, 1978Prohibits transfer of land granted to SC/ST families
Registration Act 1908 · Transfer of Property Act 1882Central laws for registering and transferring property
Land acquisitionRFCTLARR Act, 2013 (fair compensation, rehabilitation)
Forest Rights Act 2006 · Disaster Management Act 2005Forest dwellers' rights · NDMA (PM) / KSDMA (CM)
BhoomiKarnataka's computerised land records (online RTC) — a national model since the early 2000s
KaveriOnline property registration at Sub-Registrar offices
Mojini · DishankSurvey requests and sketches · app showing survey-number boundaries on a map
Nadakacheri (Atalji Janasnehi Kendra)Hobli-level centres for caste, income and residence certificates
FRUITSFarmer Registration and Unified beneficiary InformaTion System (one farmer ID for all schemes)

3.6 British land revenue systems (asked in 2024 and 2026)

SystemWho / whenWhereSettlement with
Permanent Settlement (Zamindari)Lord Cornwallis, 1793Bengal, Bihar, OdishaZamindar (middleman); revenue fixed for ever
RyotwariThomas Munro (with Alexander Read), 1820Madras and Bombay presidencies (most of South India incl. Karnataka)Directly with the ryot (cultivator)
MahalwariHolt Mackenzie, 1822 (revised 1833)North-Western Provinces, Punjab, central IndiaThe village (mahal) as a unit
Memory hookZ = Zamindar middleman (Bengal), R = Ryot farmer (South), M = Mahal village (North-West).
TrapStatements like "Permanent Settlement was introduced in southern India" or "zamindar rights were not hereditary" are false — it was in the east, and zamindars became hereditary owners.
Self-test

  2. 1 acre = how many guntas? (1) 20 (2) 40 (3) 43 (4) 100
  3. Ryotwari settlement was made with: (1) zamindars (2) village communities (3) individual cultivators (4) temples
  4. "Land to the tiller" amendment in Karnataka was made in: (1) 1961 (2) 1965 (3) 1974 (4) 2020
  5. Updating ownership in land records after a sale is called: (1) Akarband (2) Mutation (3) Jamabandi (4) Darkhast
  6. Karnataka's online land records system: (1) Kaveri (2) Bhoomi (3) Mojini (4) Sakala
  7. Annual audit of village accounts is: (1) Jamabandi (2) Mahazar (3) Tippan (4) Pahani
  8. Permanent Settlement was introduced by: (1) Munro (2) Cornwallis (3) Mackenzie (4) Wellesley

Answers: 1-2, 2-2, 3-3, 4-3, 5-2, 6-2, 7-1, 8-2

4Indian History

About 15 questions per GK paper come from history and culture, many in "arrange chronologically" or "match the following" form — so learn the order and the pairs.

4.1 Indus Valley (Harappan) civilisation, c. 2600–1900 BCE

SiteWhereFamous for
HarappaOn the Ravi, Punjab (Pakistan) — excavated by Daya Ram Sahni, 1921Granaries; first site found (gives the civilisation its name)
Mohenjo-daroOn the Indus, Sindh — R.D. Banerji, 1922Great Bath, bronze "dancing girl", "priest-king"
LothalGujaratDockyard
KalibanganRajasthan (Ghaggar)Ploughed field, fire altars
DholaviraKutch, GujaratWater management; UNESCO World Heritage Site (2021)
RakhigarhiHaryanaLargest Harappan site in India

Planned cities with grid streets and covered drains; script still undeciphered; no evidence of iron.

4.2 Vedic age, religions and early empires

  • Rigveda is the oldest Veda (10 mandalas, 1,028 hymns); the Gayatri Mantra is in it. Sama Veda = melodies; Yajur Veda = rituals; Atharva Veda = spells and medicine. "Satyameva Jayate" comes from the Mundaka Upanishad.
  • Buddha: born at Lumbini, enlightenment at Bodh Gaya, first sermon at Sarnath ("turning the wheel of dharma"), died at Kushinagar. Buddhist councils: Rajagriha → Vaishali → Pataliputra (under Ashoka) → Kashmir (under Kanishka).
  • Jainism: 24 tirthankaras, first Rishabhanatha, last Mahavira. Chandragupta Maurya went with Bhadrabahu to Shravanabelagola.
  • Mauryas: Chandragupta Maurya (c. 321 BCE) with Chanakya (Arthashastra); Greek envoy Megasthenes wrote Indica. Ashoka turned to dhamma after the Kalinga war (c. 261 BCE); edicts mostly in Brahmi. His Lion Capital at Sarnath is India's national emblem.
  • Ashokan edicts in Karnataka: Maski (Raichur — the first edict found that names "Ashoka"), Brahmagiri, Siddapura, Jatinga-Rameshwara (Chitradurga), Gavimath and Palkigundu (Koppal), Sannati (Kalaburagi).
  • Kushanas: Kanishka; the Saka era (78 CE) is India's national calendar; Gandhara and Mathura schools of art.
  • Guptas (from 320 CE), the "golden age": Samudragupta (Allahabad pillar inscription by Harisena); Chandragupta II Vikramaditya (Chinese pilgrim Fa-Hien; Kalidasa); Aryabhata (Aryabhatiya). Nalanda university was founded under Kumaragupta I.
  • Harsha of Kannauj (606–647): Chinese pilgrim Hiuen Tsang; was defeated on the Narmada by Pulakeshin II of the Badami Chalukyas.
  • Cholas: Rajaraja I built the Brihadeeswara temple at Thanjavur; Rajendra I sent naval expeditions to South-East Asia; Uttaramerur inscriptions describe village self-government.

4.3 Delhi Sultanate and Mughals

Ruler / dynastyRemember
Slave dynasty (1206)Qutbuddin Aibak (began Qutub Minar); Iltutmish; Razia Sultan — first woman ruler of Delhi; Balban
KhaljiAlauddin Khalji — market control, standing army; Malik Kafur raided the South
TughlaqMuhammad bin Tughlaq — moved capital to Daulatabad, token copper currency; Ibn Battuta visited
LodiIbrahim Lodi lost the First Battle of Panipat (1526) to Babur
Babur → HumayunSher Shah Suri interrupted Humayun's rule: Grand Trunk Road, silver rupiya
AkbarSecond Battle of Panipat (1556, against Hemu); mansabdari system; Din-i-Ilahi; Todar Mal's land revenue reforms
Shah Jahan · AurangzebTaj Mahal, Red Fort · empire at its largest, then decline
MarathasShivaji crowned 1674 at Raigad; Third Battle of Panipat (1761): Ahmad Shah Abdali defeated the Marathas

1857 revolt: began at Meerut on 10 May 1857 (Mangal Pandey's protest at Barrackpore came earlier, in March). Leaders: Bahadur Shah Zafar (Delhi), Rani Lakshmibai (Jhansi), Nana Saheb and Tantia Tope (Kanpur), Begum Hazrat Mahal (Lucknow), Kunwar Singh (Bihar). Result: Government of India Act 1858 — Crown rule.

5Karnataka: History, Culture and Unification

Karnataka-specific questions were the biggest single block in the VAO 2024 GK paper (22 of 100). Learn the dynasty table cold.

5.1 Dynasties of Karnataka

5.2 Hyder Ali, Tipu Sultan and the Anglo-Mysore wars

WarYearsEnded with
First1767–69Treaty of Madras (Hyder Ali won)
Second1780–84Treaty of Mangalore (Hyder died 1782; Tipu continued)
Third1790–92Treaty of Srirangapatna — Tipu lost half his kingdom (Cornwallis)
Fourth1799Tipu killed at Srirangapatna (4 May 1799, Wellesley); Wodeyars restored under Subsidiary Alliance

Tipu, the "Tiger of Mysore": Mysorean iron rockets, promoted sericulture, new calendar and coinage. After 1831 Mysore was under British Commissioners (Mark Cubbon); the Rendition of 1881 returned it to the Wodeyars.

Modern Mysore under Nalvadi Krishnaraja Wodeyar IVAsia's first hydro-electric station at Shivanasamudra (1902) · Bank of Mysore (1913) · University of Mysore (1916) · Kannada Sahitya Parishat (1915) · Bhadravati iron works (1923) · KRS dam (Kaveri) — all with Diwan Sir M. Visvesvaraya (Diwan 1912–18; Bharat Ratna 1955; Engineers' Day 15 September).

5.6 Culture

Folk arts

  • Yakshagana — coastal dance-drama (Tenkutittu, Badagutittu)
  • Dollu Kunitha — drum dance of the Kurubas
  • Veeragase · Kamsale · Somana Kunitha
  • Bhootha Kola — spirit worship of Tulunadu
  • Togalu Gombeyata — leather puppetry
  • Krishna Parijata — north Karnataka theatre
  • Kambala — buffalo race (coast); Huli Vesha — tiger dance

Festivals

  • Mysuru Dasara — Nadahabba; Jamboo Savari (Chamundeshwari on the golden howdah)
  • Bengaluru Karaga · Hampi Utsav · Ugadi
  • Kodagu: Huttari (harvest), Kailpodh, Kaveri Sankramana at Talakaveri

Music

  • Hindustani greats from the Dharwad region: Bhimsen Joshi (Bharat Ratna), Gangubai Hangal, Mallikarjun Mansur, Basavaraj Rajguru

Crafts with GI tags: Mysore silk · Mysore sandal soap · Channapatna toys · Bidriware (Bidar; silver inlay on dark alloy) · Ilkal sarees · Kasuti embroidery · Navalgund durries · Udupi mallige · Coorg orange · Nanjangud banana · Dharwad pedha.

UNESCO World Heritage in Karnataka: Hampi (1986), Pattadakal (1987), Hoysala temples (2023) and the Western Ghats (natural, 2012).

People: Field Marshal K.M. Cariappa (Kodagu; first Indian Commander-in-Chief, 15 Jan 1949 → Army Day) · General K.S. Thimayya · Sir M. Visvesvaraya and C.N.R. Rao (Bharat Ratna) · Sri Shivakumara Swamiji of Siddaganga.

Self-test

  2. Capital of the Rashtrakutas: (1) Badami (2) Manyakheta (3) Kalyani (4) Banavasi
  3. Battle of Talikota was fought in: (1) 1336 (2) 1509 (3) 1565 (4) 1610
  4. Treaty that ended the Third Anglo-Mysore War: (1) Madras (2) Mangalore (3) Srirangapatna (4) Salbai
  5. Vidurashwatha tragedy took place in: (1) 1924 (2) 1938 (3) 1942 (4) 1947
  6. Mysore State was renamed Karnataka on: (1) 1 Nov 1956 (2) 26 Jan 1950 (3) 1 Nov 1973 (4) 15 Aug 1972
  7. Gol Gumbaz is in: (1) Bidar (2) Kalaburagi (3) Vijayapura (4) Hampi
  8. Who is called "Karnataka Kula Purohita"? (1) Alur Venkata Rao (2) Gangadhar Rao Deshpande (3) Hardekar Manjappa (4) B.M. Srikantaiah

6Geography of India and Karnataka, with Environment

Expect 6–10 questions: rivers and dams, national parks, soils and crops, plus environment conventions and Karnataka's own issues.

6.1 India at a glance

ItemFact
Area32.87 lakh km² — 7th largest country (2.4% of world area); most populous country
Extent8°4′N–37°6′N, 68°7′E–97°25′E
Standard meridian82°30′E (near Mirzapur) → IST = GMT + 5:30
Tropic of Cancer passes through 8 statesGujarat, Rajasthan, MP, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, Mizoram (not Karnataka)
Southernmost pointIndira Point (Great Nicobar); mainland: Kanyakumari
Longest land borderWith Bangladesh
States / UTs28 states, 8 Union Territories
Largest / smallest stateRajasthan / Goa (by area); Uttar Pradesh most populous

6.2 Physical features

  • Himalaya: Himadri (Greater), Himachal (Lesser), Shiwalik (outer). Highest peak within India: Kangchenjunga.
  • Western Ghats (Sahyadri): continuous, higher, heavy rain on the windward side. Eastern Ghats: broken, lower. Highest peak of the peninsula: Anamudi (Kerala).
  • West coast: Konkan · Kanara (Karavali) · Malabar. East coast: Northern Circars · Coromandel.
  • Barren Island (Andamans) is India's only active volcano; Lakshadweep islands are coral.

6.3 Rivers

RiverRemember
GangaBhagirathi + Alaknanda meet at Devprayag; longest river in India; national river
BrahmaputraTsangpo in Tibet; Majuli (Assam) is the largest river island
GodavariLongest peninsular river — "Dakshina Ganga"
KrishnaRises at Mahabaleshwar; tributaries Bhima, Ghataprabha, Malaprabha, Tungabhadra
KaveriRises at Talakaveri (Brahmagiri, Kodagu); tributaries Harangi, Hemavathi, Kabini, Shimsha, Arkavathi, Lakshmanatirtha; east to the Bay of Bengal
Narmada and TapiWest-flowing through rift valleys; form estuaries, not deltas

Water disputes: Kaveri — tribunal 1990, final award 2007, Supreme Court verdict 2018, Cauvery Water Management Authority (2018). Mahadayi/Mhadei (Karnataka–Goa–Maharashtra) — tribunal award 2018. Krishna — Bachawat (1976) and Brijesh Kumar (2010) tribunals.

6.4 Climate, soils and crops

  • South-west monsoon (June–September) gives most rain; the north-east (retreating) monsoon (October–December) rains on the Tamil Nadu coast. Wettest place: Mawsynram. In Karnataka: Agumbe, the "Cherrapunji of the South".
  • Soils: alluvial (most widespread, plains) · black/regur (cotton soil, from Deccan lava; north Karnataka) · red (most of south Karnataka) · laterite (heavy rain, leached; Malnad and coast).
  • Seasons: Kharif (June–Oct: rice, ragi, jowar, maize, cotton, groundnut) · Rabi (Oct–Mar: wheat, gram, mustard) · Zaid (summer: watermelon, cucumber).
  • Revolutions: Green (food grains; M.S. Swaminathan) · White (milk; Verghese Kurien) · Blue (fish) · Yellow (oilseeds) · Golden (horticulture) · Silver (eggs) · Round (potato) · Grey (fertilisers).

6.5 Karnataka physical geography

ItemFact
Area1,91,791 km² (6th largest state); 31 districts; about 320 km of coast
NeighboursGoa, Maharashtra, Telangana, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu, Kerala + Arabian Sea
RegionsKaravali (coast: Dakshina Kannada, Udupi, Uttara Kannada) · Malnad (Western Ghats) · north and south plateau (Bayaluseeme)
Highest peakMullayanagiri (about 1,930 m, Baba Budangiri, Chikkamagaluru). Highest in Kodagu: Tadiandamol
Nandi HillsChikkaballapur — source of the Arkavathi and Pennar rivers
State symbolsAnimal: elephant · bird: Indian roller · tree: sandalwood · flower: lotus

Wildlife

  • National parks (5): Bandipur, Nagarahole (Rajiv Gandhi), Bannerghatta, Kudremukh, Anshi.
  • Tiger reserves (5): Bandipur (1973–74, among the first), Nagarahole, Bhadra, Kali (Dandeli–Anshi), BRT (Biligiri Rangaswamy Temple).
  • Tiger census 2022: Karnataka 563 — second after Madhya Pradesh (785). Karnataka has India's largest elephant population.
  • Bird sanctuaries: Ranganathittu (Mandya), Kokkarebellur (painted storks), Gudavi, Bonal, Attiveri, Magadi.
  • Ramsar sites in Karnataka: Ranganathittu (2022), Ankasamudra, Aghanashini estuary and Magadi Kere (2024).

Economy on the map

  • Karnataka leads India in coffee (Kodagu, Chikkamagaluru, Hassan), raw silk (Ramanagara cocoon market), ragi and areca nut; famous for sandalwood.
  • Gold: Hutti (Raichur) is working; Kolar Gold Fields closed in 2001. Iron ore: Ballari–Hosapete–Sandur (Kudremukh closed). Manganese: Sandur.
  • Power: Raichur thermal · Kaiga nuclear (Uttara Kannada) · Sharavathi hydel · Pavagada solar park (Tumakuru).
  • Ports: New Mangalore (major port); Karwar naval base (INS Kadamba).
  • Industry: Bengaluru — IT capital, HAL (1940), BEL, BEML, ISRO headquarters; Bhadravati — Visvesvaraya Iron and Steel.

6.6 Environment

Convention / eventRemember
Stockholm Conference 1972First UN environment conference → UNEP; 5 June World Environment Day
Ramsar Convention 1971Wetlands (Ramsar, Iran); 2 February World Wetlands Day
CITES 1973Trade in endangered species
Vienna 1985 · Montreal Protocol 1987Ozone layer (CFCs); 16 September Ozone Day
Earth Summit, Rio 1992UNFCCC (climate), CBD (biodiversity), Agenda 21
Kyoto Protocol 1997 · Paris Agreement 2015Emission targets · keep warming well below 2 °C (aim 1.5 °C)
Cartagena 2000 · Nagoya 2010Biosafety (GM organisms) · access and benefit sharing
Recent COPsCOP28 Dubai (2023), COP29 Baku (2024), COP30 Belém, Brazil (2025)
  • Indian laws: Wildlife Protection Act 1972 · Water Act 1974 · Forest Conservation Act 1980 · Air Act 1981 · Environment Protection Act 1986 (after Bhopal) · Biological Diversity Act 2002 · National Green Tribunal 2010.
  • Movements: Chipko (1973, Uttarakhand, Sunderlal Bahuguna) · Appiko (1983, Uttara Kannada, Panduranga Hegde) — Karnataka's Chipko · Narmada Bachao Andolan (Medha Patkar) · Silent Valley (Kerala).
  • Greenhouse gases: CO₂, methane, nitrous oxide, water vapour, CFCs. Acid rain: SO₂ and NOₓ. India's biodiversity hotspots: Himalaya, Indo-Burma, Western Ghats and Sri Lanka, Sundaland (Nicobar).
  • Western Ghats reports: Madhav Gadgil (2011) and K. Kasturirangan (2013) on ecologically sensitive areas.
  • Karnataka issues: illegal mining in Ballari, Kaveri and Mahadayi disputes, Bengaluru's lakes (Bellandur froth), elephant–human conflict, sand mining; Ettinahole project to carry drinking water to Kolar and Chikkaballapur.

  3. Almatti dam is built across: (1) Kaveri (2) Tungabhadra (3) Krishna (4) Bhima

  5. Which of these is NOT a tiger reserve in Karnataka? (1) Bhadra (2) BRT (3) Ranganathittu (4) Kali

  7. Montreal Protocol concerns: (1) wetlands (2) ozone layer (3) biosafety (4) climate change
  8. Highest peak in Karnataka: (1) Kudremukh (2) Mullayanagiri (3) Tadiandamol (4) Kodachadri

7Everyday Science and Technology

About 15 questions per GK paper. KEA mixes direct facts (vitamins, instruments) with "which statements are correct" questions on light, sound and the human body.

7.1 Physics

Units and instruments

  • SI base units (7): metre, kilogram, second, ampere, kelvin, mole, candela.
  • Force — newton; work/energy — joule; power — watt; pressure — pascal; frequency — hertz; charge — coulomb; resistance — ohm.
  • Barometer — air pressure · Hygrometer — humidity · Anemometer — wind speed · Lactometer — milk purity · Hydrometer — liquid density · Pyrometer — very high temperature · Seismograph — earthquakes · Sphygmomanometer — blood pressure · Odometer — distance travelled · Ammeter/Voltmeter — current/voltage.
  • °F = 9/5 × °C + 32 · K = °C + 273.15 · −40 °C = −40 °F · body temperature 37 °C = 98.6 °F.

Laws and formulas

  • Newton: inertia · F = ma · action–reaction (rocket, recoil of a gun).
  • Work W = F × d (lifting: W = mgh) · Power P = W / t.
  • Ohm's law V = IR · Power P = VI. Fuse: low melting point, in series. Indian mains: about 230 V, 50 Hz AC.
  • Archimedes (buoyancy) · Pascal (hydraulic press) · Bernoulli (aircraft lift) · Hooke (elasticity).
Example (asked by KEA, Feb 2026)A lifts 10 kg by 1 m in 3 s; B lifts 5 kg by 2 m in 4 s. Work: both 100 J (equal). Power: A = 100/3 ≈ 33 W, B = 100/4 = 25 W → A delivered more power.
  • Light: reflection (plane mirror image is virtual, same size) · refraction (pencil looks bent in water) · total internal reflection (optical fibre, diamond sparkle, mirage) · dispersion (prism, rainbow: dispersion + refraction + internal reflection) · scattering (blue sky, red sunset). Raman effect: C.V. Raman, Nobel 1930; National Science Day 28 February.
  • Eye: myopia (near-sightedness) → concave lens; hypermetropia (far-sightedness) → convex lens; presbyopia → bifocal.
  • Sound: needs a medium (no sound in vacuum); fastest in solids, then liquids, slowest in gases (about 343 m/s in air). Human range 20 Hz–20 kHz; ultrasound above 20 kHz (SONAR, bats); infrasound below 20 Hz (elephants, earthquakes).
  • Catching a cricket ball while pulling the hands back increases the time of impact, reducing the force (asked by KEA, Jan 2026).

7.2 Chemistry

Common nameChemicalCommon nameChemical
Common saltNaClBaking sodaNaHCO₃ (sodium hydrogen carbonate)
Washing sodaNa₂CO₃·10H₂OBleaching powderCaOCl₂
Plaster of ParisCaSO₄·½H₂OGypsumCaSO₄·2H₂O
Limestone / marble / chalkCaCO₃Quicklime · slaked limeCaO · Ca(OH)₂
Dry iceSolid CO₂Laughing gasN₂O
Marsh gas · CNGMethane (CH₄)LPGPropane + butane (ethyl mercaptan added for smell)
Welding gasAcetylene (C₂H₂)Baking powderBaking soda + tartaric acid
  • pH: below 7 acidic, 7 neutral, above 7 basic. Stomach — HCl; vinegar — acetic acid; lemon — citric; tamarind — tartaric; curd — lactic; ant sting — formic; apple — malic; tomato — oxalic. Antacid: milk of magnesia (base).
  • Air: nitrogen 78%, oxygen 21%, argon about 0.9%, CO₂ about 0.04%. Most abundant element in the Earth's crust: oxygen; most abundant metal: aluminium.
  • Mercury is the liquid metal; tungsten (highest melting point) is used for bulb filaments; galvanisation = zinc coating to prevent rusting.
  • Alloys: brass (Cu + Zn), bronze (Cu + Sn), stainless steel (Fe + Cr + Ni), solder (Pb + Sn), German silver (Cu + Zn + Ni), amalgam (mercury alloy).
  • Carbon forms: diamond (hardest natural substance), graphite (conducts electricity; pencils, lubricant), fullerenes, graphene.
  • Electron — J.J. Thomson (1897) · nucleus — Rutherford (1911) · neutron — Chadwick (1932). Periodic table: Mendeleev (1869); modern table arranged by atomic number.
  • Food preservation: oil samples are stored under inert gases (helium, nitrogen) to stop oxidation (rancidity) — asked by KEA (May 2026).

7.3 Biology and health

VitaminChemical nameDeficiency disease
ARetinolNight blindness
B1ThiamineBeriberi
B3NiacinPellagra
B12CyanocobalaminPernicious anaemia
CAscorbic acidScurvy (bleeding gums)
DCalciferolRickets
ETocopherolReproductive problems (antioxidant)
KPhylloquinoneBlood does not clot

Fat-soluble: A, D, E, K. Water-soluble: B and C. Iodine deficiency → goitre; iron → anaemia; protein → kwashiorkor.

Human body

  • Adult skeleton: 206 bones; longest: femur; smallest: stapes (ear).
  • Largest organ: skin; largest gland: liver; pancreas makes insulin (diabetes); thyroid makes thyroxine; pituitary = "master gland"; adrenaline = "fight or flight".
  • RBC live about 120 days (made in bone marrow); haemoglobin carries oxygen; WBC fight infection; platelets help clotting.
  • Universal donor: O negative; universal recipient: AB positive.
  • Normal BP 120/80 mm Hg; pulse about 72/min.

Diseases

  • Bacterial: tuberculosis, cholera, typhoid, tetanus, diphtheria, leprosy, plague.
  • Viral: polio, measles, mumps, chickenpox, rabies, hepatitis, dengue, influenza, AIDS, COVID-19.
  • Protozoan: malaria (Plasmodium, female Anopheles mosquito), kala-azar (sandfly).
  • Fungal: ringworm, athlete's foot.
  • Vaccines/medicine: Edward Jenner — smallpox vaccine · Louis Pasteur — rabies vaccine · Alexander Fleming — penicillin (1928) · BCG — tuberculosis.
  • Cell discovered by Robert Hooke (1665). Mitochondria = powerhouse; ribosomes make proteins; chloroplasts do photosynthesis; lysosomes = "suicide bags".
  • Photosynthesis: CO₂ + water + sunlight (chlorophyll) → glucose + O₂. Xylem carries water; phloem carries food. Plant hormones: auxin (growth), gibberellin, cytokinin, ethylene (ripens fruit), abscisic acid (stress).
  • Golden rice is genetically modified for more vitamin A (beta-carotene).

7.4 Space, defence and technology

GaganyaanIndia's human spaceflight programme. Shubhanshu Shukla flew to the International Space Station on Axiom-4 (2025)

8Economy, Banking and Government Schemes

KEA asks basic economics, banking history and — every year — schemes: who gets what, from which year.

8.1 Concepts in one line each

TermMeaning
GDPValue of all final goods and services produced within the country in a year
InflationGeneral rise in prices. CPI (retail, used by RBI for its target) · WPI (wholesale)
Repo rateRate at which RBI lends to banks (raise it to fight inflation)
CRR · SLRShare of deposits banks keep with RBI as cash · in liquid assets (gold, government securities)
Fiscal deficitTotal expenditure − total receipts (excluding borrowings)
Direct tax · indirect taxIncome tax, corporate tax · GST, customs
Budget"Annual Financial Statement" (Art 112). Presented on 1 February since 2017; railway budget merged in 2017. First budget of free India: R.K. Shanmukham Chetty (1947)

Mergers: Vijaya → Bank of Baroda (2019); Syndicate → Canara and Corporation → Union Bank (2020); State Bank of Mysore → SBI (2017).

9Mental Ability and Aptitude (about 11 marks)

The VAO 2024 GK paper had 11 aptitude questions in a row (Q51–61), and every KEA GK paper since has a similar block. These are the cheapest marks in Paper 1 — but only if you use formulas instead of trial and error.

9.1 Numbers

Divisibility3 or 9: digit sum divisible by 3/9 · 4: last two digits · 8: last three digits · 6: by 2 and 3 · 11: (sum of odd-place digits − sum of even-place digits) is 0 or a multiple of 11. For two numbers: LCM × HCF = product of the numbers.
Example (asked by KEA, May 2026)Least number that leaves remainder 3 when divided by 5, 7 and 9, but is divisible by 6: LCM(5, 7, 9) = 315 → numbers are 315k + 3. k = 1 gives 318 = 6 × 53. Answer 318.

9.2 Percentages, profit and interest

SituationShortcut
Two successive changes of a% and b%Net change = a + b + ab/100 (use minus signs for decreases)
Price rises r%; keep spending the sameCut consumption by r/(100 + r) × 100% (price +25% → cut 20%)
Profit %(SP − CP)/CP × 100. SP = CP × (100 + P%)/100
Two items sold at the same price, one at x% gain and one at x% lossAlways a loss of x²/100 % (10% and 10% → 1% loss)
Simple interestSI = P × R × T / 100
Compound interestAmount = P(1 + R/100)^T. For 2 years: CI − SI = P(R/100)²
Example₹10,000 at 10% for 2 years: SI = ₹2,000; CI = ₹2,100; difference = 10,000 × (0.1)² = ₹100.

9.3 Averages, ratios and ages

Example (asked by KEA, Jan 2026)Five years ago the average age of 4 people was 45. Sita joins now and the average of all 5 becomes 49. Today the 4 people total 4 × (45 + 5) = 200; all five total 5 × 49 = 245 → Sita is 45.
  • Average of the first n natural numbers = (n + 1)/2. If every value rises by k, the average rises by k.

9.4 Time and work, speed and distance

SituationFormula
A alone takes a days, B alone b daysTogether: ab/(a + b) days (10 and 15 → 6 days)
PipesAdd filling rates, subtract emptying rates (as fractions of the tank per hour)
Unit conversionkm/h × 5/18 = m/s (72 km/h = 20 m/s; 54 km/h = 15 m/s)
Train passes a pole / a platformTime = train length / speed · (train + platform) / speed
Relative speedSame direction: subtract · opposite directions: add
Average speed over equal distances at x and y2xy/(x + y) (40 and 60 → 48 km/h, not 50)
ExampleA 150 m train at 54 km/h (15 m/s) passes a pole in 150/15 = 10 s and a 300 m platform in 450/15 = 30 s.

9.5 Counting and probability

  • Arrangements of n things: n!. With repeats: n!/(p! q! …). MISSISSIPPI = 11!/(4! 4! 2!) = 34,650 (asked by KEA, Apr 2026).
  • Choosing r from n: nCr = n!/(r!(n − r)!). Handshakes among n people = nC2 (10 people → 45).
  • Probability = favourable/total. Two dice: 36 outcomes; P(sum 7) = 6/36 = 1/6.

9.6 Reasoning

TypeMethodExample
Number seriesCheck differences, squares/cubes, ×2, alternate patterns2, 6, 12, 20, 30, ? = n(n+1) → 42
Coding–decodingLetter positions A=1…Z=26 (EJOTY = 5, 10, 15, 20, 25); opposite pairs add to 27 (A–Z, B–Y)CAT = 3-1-20 → DOG = 4-15-7
Blood relationsDraw the family tree; mark males □ and females ○"He is the son of my grandfather's only son" → my brother
DirectionsRight turn = clockwise; use Pythagoras for the shortest distance3 km north then 4 km east → 5 km north-east of start
RankingTotal = rank from top + rank from bottom − 17th from top, 13th from bottom → 19
CalendarOdd days: ordinary year 1, leap year 2; 100 yrs 5, 200 yrs 3, 300 yrs 1, 400 yrs 04 Oct 2026 is Sunday → 4 Oct 2027 is Monday
ClocksAngle = |30H − 5.5M| (if above 180°, subtract from 360°). Hands overlap 11 times in 12 hours; right angle 22 times in 12 hours3:40 → |90 − 220| = 130°
SyllogismsDraw the smallest Venn diagram that fits; a conclusion follows only if it is true in every possible diagramAll A are B, some B are C → no definite conclusion about A and C
Seating (circle, facing centre)Fix one person, place the definite clues first; "left" means clockwise for people facing the centre
Self-test
  1. 20% of 30% of 500 = (1) 25 (2) 30 (3) 50 (4) 300
  2. A finishes a job in 12 days, B in 24. Together? (1) 6 (2) 8 (3) 9 (4) 18 days
  3. 72 km/h = ? m/s (1) 18 (2) 20 (3) 25 (4) 30
  4. CP ₹800, SP ₹1,000. Profit %? (1) 20 (2) 25 (3) 30 (4) 200
  5. 1, 4, 9, 16, 25, ? (1) 30 (2) 35 (3) 36 (4) 49
  6. Angle between the hands at 9:00 (1) 60° (2) 90° (3) 120° (4) 270°
  7. Ravi is 10th from the left and 15th from the right. How many in the row? (1) 23 (2) 24 (3) 25 (4) 26
  8. Arrangements of the letters of LEVEL (1) 20 (2) 30 (3) 60 (4) 120

Answers: 1-2, 2-2, 3-2, 4-2, 5-3, 6-2, 7-2, 8-2

11ಕನ್ನಡ ವ್ಯಾಕರಣ (Kannada Grammar)

Paper 2 Q1–35. In 2024 about 19 of the 35 Kannada questions were grammar: ಸಂಧಿ, ಸಮಾಸ, ವಿಭಕ್ತಿ, ಕ್ರಿಯಾಪದ, ತತ್ಸಮ-ತದ್ಭವ, ಅಲಂಕಾರ, ಛಂದಸ್ಸು, ನುಡಿಗಟ್ಟು. Grammar is pure pattern recognition — learn the rule, then read the example tables until the answers feel obvious.

11.1 ವರ್ಣಮಾಲೆ (Alphabet)

ನಿಯಮಕನ್ನಡ ವರ್ಣಮಾಲೆಯಲ್ಲಿ 49 ಅಕ್ಷರಗಳು: ಸ್ವರ 13 (ಅ ಆ ಇ ಈ ಉ ಊ ಋ ಎ ಏ ಐ ಒ ಓ ಔ) + ಯೋಗವಾಹ 2 (ಅಂ – ಅನುಸ್ವಾರ, ಅಃ – ವಿಸರ್ಗ) + ವ್ಯಂಜನ 34 (ವರ್ಗೀಯ 25 + ಅವರ್ಗೀಯ 9: ಯ ರ ಲ ವ ಶ ಷ ಸ ಹ ಳ).
  • ಹ್ರಸ್ವ ಸ್ವರ (1 ಮಾತ್ರೆ): ಅ ಇ ಉ ಋ ಎ ಒ · ದೀರ್ಘ ಸ್ವರ (2 ಮಾತ್ರೆ): ಆ ಈ ಊ ಏ ಐ ಓ ಔ · ಪ್ಲುತ (3 ಮಾತ್ರೆ): ಕರೆಯುವಾಗ.
  • ವರ್ಗೀಯ ವ್ಯಂಜನ: ಕ ವರ್ಗ, ಚ ವರ್ಗ, ಟ ವರ್ಗ, ತ ವರ್ಗ, ಪ ವರ್ಗ. ಪ್ರತಿ ವರ್ಗದ 1, 3ನೇ ಅಕ್ಷರ ಅಲ್ಪಪ್ರಾಣ (ಕ ಗ), 2, 4ನೇ ಮಹಾಪ್ರಾಣ (ಖ ಘ), 5ನೇ ಅನುನಾಸಿಕ (ಙ ಞ ಣ ನ ಮ).
ಉಚ್ಚಾರಣಾ ಸ್ಥಾನವರ್ಣಗಳು
ಕಂಠ್ಯ (ಗಂಟಲು)ಅ ಆ, ಕ ಖ ಗ ಘ ಙ, ಹ

ದಂತ್ಯ (ಹಲ್ಲು)ತ ಥ ದ ಧ ನ, ಲ ಸ
ಓಷ್ಠ್ಯ (ತುಟಿ)ಉ ಊ, ಪ ಫ ಬ ಭ ಮ

ಕಂಠೋಷ್ಠ್ಯಒ ಓ ಔ
